> **LEAD 13 (B+/A−, kernel_opt, own-rule-or-rider at composition): prism `8eaee8b7f` #107 'cuda: fold the MoE router weighting and expert reduction into one kernel'** — the never-materialize weighted-reduce fusion. Commit body read verbatim via the API this session: 'The mul writes a second full-size F32 tensor that only exists so the add chain can read it back; at large ubatch that is tens of MB written and read again per layer. The fused kernel folds the weighting into the reduction so the weighted intermediate is never materialized: only down_out streams in and the [n_embd, n_tokens] result streams out.' — the BIT-IDENTITY instrument is the FMA-contraction refusal: 'GGML_OP_MUL rounds each product to F32 before storing it and the add chain folds left to right, so the kernel rounds every product (__fmul_rn) before accumulating (__fadd_rn) in increasing slot order; the explicit intrinsics keep nvcc from contracting a product and its accumulation into an FMA.' — plus the DYING-OPERAND STAGING RESCUE: 'The weights die at the mul, so the graph allocator routinely packs the add-chain output over them; rather than give up the fusion the op snapshots them into pool scratch (they are n_expert_used*n_tokens floats). An x/dst overlap still rejects the fusion.' — cap + switches: expert counts 2..15 fuse / 16 falls through to the unfused ops; GGML_CUDA_MOE_REDUCE_DISABLE=1 + MOE_REDUCE_DEBUG both parsed beside GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION; tests carry 0.0-error-bound bit-identity + hand-placed buffer-aliasing cases (weights inside dst range → must stage; dst inside x range → must decline). THE MECHANISM: a 4th q8_1 activation layout `GGML_CUDA_Q8_1_PQ2` — qs bytes permuted INSIDE each 16-element group (position k*4+m holds element m*4+k) + half2 (d, int16 sum) per 32-block → `(code_word >> 2k) & 0x03030303` pairs RAW 2-bit codes with activation bytes so **dp4a needs NO per-weight decode** (one shift+mask yields four dp4a-ready bytes) and the ternary digit bias is ONE integer subtraction via the ACTIVATION's stored sum — 'sum(q*(c-1)) = sum(q*c) - sum(q)' (qsum already lives in block_q8_1; the correction rides existing data, no per-weight (c-1) decode); warp = 4 rows reusing the activation slice (PQ2_0_MC_ROWS 4 × PQ2_0_MC_WARPS 4); `__launch_bounds__(128, 3)` register cap = 3 blocks/SM; admission predicate: Ada-only (`cc == GGML_CUDA_CC_ADA_LOVELACE`), plain 2D, no ids, not batch-invariant, 3-8 columns, per-ncols template switch 3..8. the PERF content is the chescock review thread 2026-09-21, applied by the author in-PR)** — THE LAW: a runtime bool branching around a large inlinable body forces the inliner into duplicate-or-deinline; restructure as a CONDITIONAL GUARD so the body compiles ONCE. chescock verbatim: 'Normally the system and `get_param` all get inlined together, but since `self.is_exclusive` is a runtime check this will have to either inline *two* copies of the system, or leave it as a non-inlined function call.' + the fix sketch verbatim: 'let _guard = self.is_exclusive.then(|| { // Set `world.last_change_tick` and create a guard to set it back }); run(world)?; — That should ensure that `run` is only compiled in once.' + the author's confirmation verbatim: 'Yep that works pretty well, changed!'. The merged shape: `if self.is_exclusive { world.last_change_tick_scope(last_run, run) } else { run(world) }` (two copies of the inlinable `run` closure) → the `LastTickGuard` + single `run` call. KINSHIP (grep-verified this pass): `inline-hint-not-through-unmarked-wrapper` (B211, the DISPATCH-axis inlining member) — different axis, compose never merge; `mir-destprop-branch-site-merge` (B99, the CALL-SITE axis) pulls the OPPOSITE direction on ITS axis (split two known call sites for per-site predictor history) — NOT a conflict: B99 is about fn-POINTER dispatch sites, Lead 10 is about ONE inlinable BODY duplicated by a surrounding branch — cite the distinction in the rule to prevent a false dedup in either direction; `branchless-conditional` is the data-level select (cmov), unrelated. Likely NotBenchable-annotated (codegen-shape claim; a microbench of the stand-in measures nothing the law asserts — the B182/186 class) or asm-inspection-verified at conversion. When both operands of the ADD are mat-mul outputs (x = W1 @ u + W2 @ v), that test is true for both, so the residual resolves to the fused mat-mul's own, never-written output and the kernel adds whatever that buffer holds' (in-commit verbatim); the failure shape: Clef decision models' head (proj_option_context @ ctx + proj_option_lexical @ lex, 9 option rows) — '/v1/systemone probabilities collapse toward uniform (billing 0.28 where the CPU backend gives 0.977 ...), deterministic per memory layout, correct with GGML_METAL_FUSION_DISABLE=1. Not a quantization issue: the same file is right on CPU'; the fix: 'The fusion check (ggml_metal_mul_mat_add_operand) already selects the operand by identity; make the encoder do the same'; the test: 'Add a MUL_MAT_ADD mode to test-backend-ops where the residual is a second mat-mul; on Metal it fails 27 of 28 cases before this change'. The law: a fusion admission that must pick ONE operand of a binary op selects by BUFFER IDENTITY, never by a negated op-predicate — negation is sound only when at most one operand can match. **LEAD 2 — llama.cpp `d2a79e6046` #29171 sycl GLM MLA prefill MKL FA admission — A− (PREFILL), kernel_opt D**: 'GLM-4.7 Flash uses an MLA shape with 576-wide Q/K heads, a 512-wide V head, GQA 20, and F16 KV. The SYCL dispatcher rejects this shape because the normal MKL flash-attention gate requires matching K/V widths and caps the head dimension at 512, so prompt processing falls back to the substantially slower TILE kernel'; the fix admits ONLY the validated exact shape ('Admit only the validated 576/576/512, GQA-20 F16 shape to the existing MKL pipeline. Keep all other mismatched K/V shapes on their current fallback paths') + the V-cache-as-strided-view-of-K aliasing conditions ('Handle GLM's V cache as a narrower strided view of K rows. Select the strided F16 descriptor when row stride is padded, and alias K/V dequantization buffers only when their logical widths match. Restrict the stride exception to a real V view sharing K's row stride') + 'Add the exact 576/512, GQA-20 prompt-path backend test. On an Intel Arc Pro B70 at master e613ef2, pp8192 improves from 432.80 to 1292.29 tok/s (2.99x, +198.6%). tg256 remains unchanged within noise at 45.67 versus 45.65 tok/s'; ⚠ IN-COMMIT NEGATIVE: the F16-scores half ('store MKL flash attention scores in F16 ... The F32 score matrix is the largest flash-attention intermediate on this path; storing it as F16 halves its size and traffic ... pp8192 1583.9 -> 1657.1 (+4.6%) pp64000 610.0 -> 684.5 (+12.2%) pp131072 ~354 -> 402.1 (+13.5%)') was REVERTED within the same merged PR ('Revert "sycl: store MKL flash attention scores in F16" — This reverts commit 265f9748164dc88e02678425b75f520ccd528b32' — revert reason unstated in-commit; measured-positive on record, landed-reverted — watch-only, never stage the F16 half without the revert reason). **LEAD 3 — llama.cpp `988190680d` #30065 metal few-row MMA remaining src0 types — B−, RIDER on fewrow-tensor-tile-decode-into-rhs-operand (B198; the B205 a3a1c47 precedent: upstream few-row MMA instances are dedup-notes, not rules)**: 'The generic few-row MMA kernel works for any type with a 16-weight dequantizer, so it now also takes BF16, Q1_0, Q2_0, MXFP4, Q2_K, Q3_K, TQ2_0 and the IQ types. Each type starts at the row count where it beats the current kernels on an M3 Ultra: 5 rows for TQ2_0, 4 for BF16, 3 for MXFP4, Q2_0, Q2_K and IQ4_NL, and 2 for the others' + the measured table ('0.23 to 0.98 from the threshold to 8 rows, 0.24 to 0.33 at 9 to 16 rows, and 0.99 to 1.01 at 1 and 512 rows', M3 Ultra source-reported, interleaved mean-of-two) — the PER-TYPE row-threshold table + the 16-weight-dequantizer generality condition are the new decision content; rider, not a rule. **LEAD 4 — llama.cpp `b9acf138a1` #29928 GLM5Next MTP headless forward — B−, kernel_opt D (own rule or rider at conversion)**: 'A NextN forward with no output rows (the MTP catch-up and the draft-context prefill) persists only through its cache writes, so the headless graph keeps the MLA latent, indexer key|gate and pooled-key writes and drops the query path, the indexer selection, the attention body, the FFN and the LM head. The 4-token catch-up falls from 6.9 ms to 0.33 ms of kernels' — then the LANDED final state CROPS instead of prunes: 'Replace the headless NextN prune with the crop pattern the other MTP graphs use: gather the attention output and the block input at the output ids before the position-wise FFN and the shared head. A NextN forward with no output rows then runs the FFN and the head over zero rows. The 4-token catch-up falls from 6.9 ms to 2.9 ms of kernels; greedy output hashes are unchanged' — the consistency-over-deep-prune tradeoff IN-COMMIT measured on both sides (0.33 prune vs 2.9 crop; crop landed for the shared pattern) + 'the greedy output hashes and the draft acceptance are unchanged' correctness pins; also the trunk/MTP-only GGUF split-load + the shared-tail rollback refusal (partial recurrent rollback whose tail cell is shared is REJECTED, never silently moved). **LEAD 5 — cubecl `9865237018` #1770 parallel compilation — C+/B−, RIDER on profile-window-drains-queued-work-before-open (B207, the drain family) + a work-stealing note**: the patch (4826 insertions/2437 deletions, full .patch read) splits compilation into KernelLoader + ArtifactCompiler (any-thread lowering) / CompilationTarget (server-thread load/store) with parallel batch compilation ('The half of the work any thread can do then runs on as many threads as there are kernels, up to the configured parallelism ... However long the queue, it compiles in one phase that lasts about as long as its slowest kernel'); the drain-family member is the measurement honesty — client.rs: 'Compile every kernel queued for compilation ..., before the work submitted after it. Every measurement starts with it — a tune, a throughput probe — so a queue a compile-only dry run left is not timed as part of the first launch measured' + tuner.rs: 'Kernels a compile-only dry run queued compile here, together, before anything is timed: left to the next launch, they would all compile inside the first candidate's warmup, and its measurement would carry every candidate's compilation'; the work-stealing law: 'kernels differ by orders of magnitude in how long they take to compile, so a fixed split would leave threads idle behind the slowest share' (for_each_at_once dynamic next-index pickup, first-panic-resumed); > **(1) `fewrow-tensor-tile-decode-into-rhs-operand` A− (lead `3285e757a` #284/#286; few-row approach adapted from the Yukon MLX.fast qmm_m16_block core, Apache-2.0 — provenance in-source): a cooperative-tensor matmul2d tile of 16 src1 rows × 32 weight rows per simdgroup at speculative verify widths, PQ2_0 weights DECODED STRAIGHT INTO the right-hand cooperative tensor ("so the K loop needs no threadgroup staging and no barriers"), because both neighbors lose — "the generic tensor mul_mm tile is 64 x 128, which computes far more columns than a 4..16 row verify batch needs, and mul_mv_ext is ALU-bound at 2..8 columns" (ops.cpp, in-source). Measured THREE-neighbor crossover with the gaps named: mat-vec < 3 | fewrow 3..32 | generic tensor > 32; "n=1..2 are slower on the new tile (150 vs 71 and 130 us), hence min 3" (commit message, M5 Pro best-of-2: n=8 484.5→157.4, n=16 855.9→172.8, n=32 864→328.3 µs @ 17408×5120). NCB×NKS template split (5 pinned host combos, `GGML_METAL_PQ2_0_FEWROW_CFG`), kill-switch `FEWROW=0`, and the tensor element-layout PROBE technique ("The cooperative tensor element layout was probed on M5 with get_multidimensional_index before writing the decode"). > **RULE LEADS (A− 4):** (1) `grammar-window-draft-prune-path-local-mask` — structured-output × DFlash2-class drafting: before the verify forward the grammar matcher walks the draft tree depth-first (`accept_token` down, `rollback` up), drops drafts NO accepted path can hold (a grammar-rejected token, a stop token, and every row under either — "the parent's masked row could never choose it"), fills each kept row's allowed-token bitmask from its path, −inf's the rest before sampling; EXACTNESS LAW row-locality: "A row's logits do not depend on the other rows, and its mask depends only on its path, so a drafted row and the serial step with the same path choose the same token, greedy or keyed" (98fb8f9, at-sha — the line moved files); bounded refusal surface (xgrammar missing / schema won't compile / TP rank-1 structured-off with packed-constraint sharing "both ranks then walk and mask the same rows", grammar.py:158); NOVEL — zero grammar/constrained-decoding content in corpus (the 8 "grammar" grep hits are B193's "name grammar" prose + proposer parser comments). (2) `affine-block-address-lets-pipeliner-fly` — layout-for-addressability: the stored-block address `(b // 4) * 32 * SBN + ((b % 4) * 16) // 2 * SBN` is a STAIRCASE in the loop step — "an address the pipeliner cannot see as affine is not put in flight" (443ad4a, at-sha) — and since a stored macro block holds 8 tile rows the same address is simply `b * (8 * SBN)`: "8 bytes a block (low nibble: even input), 8 * SBN apart: an address the pipeliner follows" (nvfp4.py:312, in-source); distinct-from hoist-bit-address-out-of-packed-row-sweep (LICM hoists an INVARIANT; this makes an ADVANCING address affine so the pipeliner overlaps it); the priority datum "the weight traffic is what the step actually waits on" (message-only). (3) `register-width-topk-falls-back-to-tiled-radix-select` — per-row selection holding `next_pow2(blocks)` scores in registers hits a SPILL cliff (`SELECT_REGS = 32768  # _select holds a row's block scores in registers up to this many; past it they spill`, attention.py:13 in-source; past it a 256-row prompt block read 26-27 ms vs 0.8 — message-only); the fix dispatches by register budget with an OUTPUT-IDENTICAL fallback: "``_select`` while ``blocks`` fit its registers, ``_select_tiles`` (the same lists) past them" (attention.py:353 in-source) — tiled radix select one byte a pass from the top, ties by lowest block id; distinct-from wide-verify-warp-specialized-scores-in-registers-pv (that CHOOSES the registers posture by width; this handles the spill side with an identical-output fallback). (4) `startup-estimate-derived-from-allocation-shapes` — the capacity plan's EXL3 term was a fixed 1.125 GiB/rank guess while the real engine allocated an `exl3_mm.Scratch` per buffer set + 1 GiB prompt split-K partials NOTHING counted → ~2.5 GiB under-count, admission lied; the fix derives every term from the allocation shapes themselves (`exl3_expert_scratch` mirrors `Scratch`'s formula, geometry.py:222-227 in-source) and proves it with a FAKE-ALLOCATOR RECONCILIATION: at real shapes, allocated ≤ estimate (3f35ea9); absorbs the estimator-blindness facets: the NVFP4 widening made byte-count estimates blind 4.00×→1.01× (3655360, message-only) and the `--checkpoint-slots` knob joins the estimate per kept state + is refused pre-download when unservable (f151c6f, serve_options.py:36 in-source) — `cache-slot-knob-budget-counted-refused-pre-download` folds here as a sub-arm; NOVEL (the 2 "reconcil/ALLOCATION SHAPE" grep hits are pad-skip prose + a B-correction note, not this mechanism).
> **B+ (8):** (5) `prefix-cache-entry-one-token-early` — a kept prompt-state entry ending AT the prompt's last token can never hit: a resume needs a STRICTLY SHORTER prefix (an identical resend, or a next turn whose final token renders differently — thinking on/off) always prefills in full; keep the entry at `max(1, len(prompt) - 1)` with a MID-CHUNK state cut (per-layer DeltaNet state + conv window captured between two launches, `Cut` forward.py:123 + `return max(1, len(prompt) - 1)` decode.py:121, both in-source) + MTP tail rows after the point; resume-equals-fresh pinned ("(``resume`` equals a fresh run); ``e.kept`` resumes prompt[:keep_at]", decode.py:316); NOVEL. (6) `dual-reduction-shared-butterfly-ladder` — the serial DeltaNet recurrence computes TWO dependent lane-sums per step (state mem + next node's read-out); BOTH ride ONE `__shfl_xor` butterfly pass — "Each node's read-out sum goes through the next node's butterfly (same order), so a step waits on one, not two." (gdn.cu:143, in-source); bit-safe (identical xor ladder); refuse when the piggybacked sum feeds the SAME step's update (true dependency); DIRECTLY relevant to our GDN chunked lanes; NOVEL (the "piggyback" grep hits are the dirty-bit ENCODING rule — different mechanism). (7) `idle-rank-doorbell-on-rendezvous-store` — idle TP rank 1 waited INSIDE the all-gather (NCCL kernel spinning + host copy thread spinning; "96% GPU util / ~210% CPU on two DGX Sparks" message-only); the fix: rank 0 sets a key in the rendezvous TCPStore NCCL ALREADY HOLDS before each request header ("the idle doorbell: rank 1 waits for each request on the rendezvous store (no store: no doorbell), not in NCCL", engine.py:295 in-source; `store.wait([key], timedelta(hours=1))` :314, consumed keys deleted, 1-hour timeout retries, other store errors raise, no-store skips silently); kin deadline-bounded-poll-of-submitted-completion-event (block-not-spin); NOVEL (the reuse-existing-store doorbell is the content). (9) `splitk-reduce-one-launch-slice-order-pinned` — a split-K fp32 face reduced by `out.copy_(part[0]); for s: out += part[s]` burns one elementwise launch per slice (31 of 32 wasted); both faces share ONE `_reduce` kernel with the SUMMATION ORDER PINNED: "The adds are the loop's own, in the loop's order, one fp32 add a slice, so the fp32 sums are bit-identical to the loop's" (443ad4a message; TIP `_reduce` docstring in-source) pinned by `test_matmul_splitk_sum_order_is_the_reduces_one`; split-k family EXTENSION — the in-kernel spellings ride as instances (qmm.cu:177 "K slices of a tile form a cluster: slice 0 adds peers' partials in slice order, as reduce_kernel does" + :202 `cluster.sync()` "peers keep their memory until slice 0 has read it", sm_90-gated `__trap()` below; exl3 linear.cu:244-246 last-block-done atomicAdd election reducing Z in split order with `__threadfence()` + `__ldcg`). (10) `declared-uncapturable-expert-declines-graphs` — a step whose dispatch reads host state (the NVFP4 grouped expert's plan `.tolist()` — a synchronizing D2H) cannot run in a capture and surfaced AFTER weight load as "operation failed due to a previous error during capture" with nothing pointing at the cause — INVISIBLE to the capture tests, which ran EMPTY plans where tolist() reads nothing (the lane-intel gem); the fix: the expert class DECLARES `capturable = False`, the engine reads the declaration, declines graphs with one loud line, decodes eagerly — "experts that read their plan on the host (NVFP4) can't be captured: decline graphs before a capture fails" (decode.py:142-144 in-source); later flipped True when the plan moved device-side (64e2374, `capturable = True     # the plan stays on the device` nvfp4_moe.py:30) — the DECLARATION is the capability axis; NOVEL. (11) `concurrent-round-parity-solo-equal` — `--parallel N, each reply equal to its solo run` (7399e7c) carried by (a) batch-invariant kernels — "rows run the same groups and K slices at any row count, so no row affects another" (qmm.py:1 in-source; Nemotron twin "A row's logits have the same bits at any row count (the row count is a runtime value)" sources.py:177) — an INSTANCE of batch-composition-invariant-grid-early-return B100; (b) the one-stream engine's stopping rule reused per stream (scheduling leg NOVEL); (c) admission counts N streams' states+caches BEFORE load and refuses with the window that fits; test matrix (16 streams, mixed lengths, greedy+keyed, drafts on/off, join/leave) message-only. (12) `quantized-stored-bytes-not-widened-patterns` — DOWNGRADED from the read agent's A−: decode-from-wire-bytes is our corpus's AMBIENT default (every q4/q8 kernel), so the GOAT framing fails; the mineable content is the widened-pattern ANTI-PATTERN (their NVFP4 table stored one uint16 bf16 pattern per weight = 4.00× checkpoint bytes; the fix holds the wire bytes — "True: the checkpoint's bytes (codes + fp8 scales), decoded in-kernel" nvfp4.py:34 in-source, "the device holds what the file holds (four times less than a widened grid)" message-only) + the field-arithmetic decode identity ("the pattern is a bf16's bits, so the bitcast is exact" :314 in-source) + the estimator facet → rider on lead 4; distinct-from B176 (licenses EXACT widening into a compute dtype — different axis: kernel-path widening vs STORAGE-form widening) + fp4-nibble-gather-lut-replicated-index-width (the decode-mechanism choice INSIDE the kernel — complementary).

- [x] **P49 — bevy `48ae9fc0a1` #26090 "fix UninitBufferVec to use shader's size for each entry instead of Rust's": the host-view-vs-GPU-stride law. GRADED 2026-10-13 (M3 idle envelope): B, RIDER-GRADE on the B211 layout family (`repr-align-register-partition-store-forwarding` — the THIRD face of its one-decision-two-costs law: one layout decision, TWO VIEWS).** The atom (quotes from the merge commit): 'The shader expects each entry to be 176 bytes. Bevy worked out each entry's size by asking Rust how big the struct is, not how big the shader says it is. Normally both answers are 176, but w/ glam's scalar-math feature on, Rust packs the struct tighter, to 168 bytes. If a render phase draws exactly one mesh, the buffer comes out 168 bytes when the shader needs at least 176, and wgpu throws the binding-size error. With two or more meshes the buffer happens to be big enough, so the bug stays hidden.' THE LAW: when a host struct feeds a GPU buffer, the per-entry stride is the CONSUMER's (shader's) declared size, never the host's `size_of` — feature flags (glam scalar-math) and WGSL alignment rules can diverge the two views; the single-entry buffer is the LOUD case, the multi-entry case the SILENT one (spare bytes absorb the shortfall). Fix bounded (one line: the stride from the shader's size). In-lane: our WGSL instance/uniform buffers (seal-view ambient quads + grass instances ride vertex-stage tags with host-fed layouts; riir-shader gamefx). NotBenchable (correctness law; upstream ships no numbers). QUEUED for the cargo-quiet conversion batch (rider on the B211 entries host + gate test; quotes re-verified at conversion time).
- [x] **P50 — llama.cpp `358a420e` #30416 "graph: pad the KV dimension of the no-cache attention to 256": pad-at-the-producer + the probe-shape-class law. GRADED 2026-10-13 (M3 idle envelope): B, RIDER-GRADE, two candidate homes (the B208 exact-shape-admission family OR the pad family — adjudicate at conversion).** The atom (quotes from the commit body): 'The no-cache path builds a [n_tokens, n_tokens] mask and passes K and V unpadded, so FLASH_ATTN_EXT sees K->ne[1] == n_tokens. The CUDA kernels for head size 512 require a multiple of FATTN_KQ_STRIDE, so the op falls back to the CPU for most lengths, and the -fa auto probe (1 token) disables flash attention for such models. Pad the mask like llama_kv_cache::get_n_kv and pad K and V with zeros up to the KV dimension of the mask.' TWO faces: (1) **PAD-AT-THE-PRODUCER** — a fast kernel's stride/alignment contract on a DYNAMIC dimension must be met where the tensors are BUILT (the graph builder pads mask + K/V with zeros to the same shape the cached path's `get_n_kv` already produces), never repaired at dispatch — and the root cause is a PATH-VARIANT divergence (the cached path padded, the no-cache VARIANT skipped it: the sibling-transcription class); (2) **PROBE-SHAPE-CLASS** — the `-fa` auto probe measured at ONE token, a degenerate shape OUTSIDE the stride contract's domain, and concluded the fast arm unsupported for the model GLOBALLY: a capability probe must probe the shape class it gates, never a degenerate point (kin: B208's validated exact-shape admission; our `att_pf_fa` p≥128 engagement predicate is the same dispatch-agreement class). Zero-pad semantics preserved (masked contributions). NotBenchable here (hs-512 is not in our serving lane; the law generalizes to any stride-contracted attention arm). QUEUED for the cargo-quiet conversion batch (rider; quotes re-verified at conversion via the clone).

> **(2) bevy `a144d280` / #26042 "Specify EntityLocation alignment to improve codegen" (merged 10-07T19:33Z, NEW past the B210 pin) — rust_perf candidate, B+ — the store-to-load-forwarding stall law.** A small struct's field alignment decides LLVM's register partition on the copy: since #25632 EntityLocation is written 8+4+4 and the first 8-byte chunk joins archetype_row with HALF of the second register — 'LLVM builds it by writing both registers to the stack and reading 8 bytes back. That read spans two pending stores and causes the store-to-load forwarding to fail.' The fix is ONE alignment attribute tipping the partition to 4+4+8 so the join happens in-register: 'Adding a `repr(align(8))` to `EntityLocation` seems to tip LLVM codegen the right way.' Measured: world_entity/50000 233.33 → 66.48 µs (x0.26); get_entity_mut_slice/20 152.1 → 100.9 ns (the 1.49× regression to parity). DEDUP: grep-verified NO rust_perf rule owns store-to-load forwarding / register-partition alignment (the hits are B207's inline-wrapper rule + the vec-layout/buffer-reuse/repr(C)-copy trio — different mechanisms). > **INTAKE ROW 2026-10-13 ~19:2x +07 (shikuwa/4090, idle-loop cycle 59 — the fresh upstream movement sweep, ~26h after the 10-12 pass; queues 1-4 verified empty first: zero workspace commits since c58's census, watch 971 no-fire 3/3 via REST).** **(1) RIDER-CANDIDATE, B-, kernel tail/staging family - llama.cpp `411d5485` #29533 vulkan "split matmul dispatch when it would exceed maxComputeWorkGroupCount" (landed 10-13 11:03Z): the SYMMETRIC TAIL ZERO-FILL law.** The chunked-K staging bug, quote verbatim: "The A side skipped the shared memory write for the k steps past end_k, while the B side was already told the bound and zero filled. The A registers are read for all BK_STEP steps, so the stale scale went into the dot product and produced NaN at k = 32. Pass the bound to block_a_to_shmem and write zeros when it is false, for every DATA_A type, mirroring the B side." The law: both operands of a tail-truncated K loop must handle the truncation IDENTICALLY - a zero-fill-vs-skip-write mismatch between A and B staging reads stale registers into the dot product (NaN at the chunk boundary, silent when full chunks dominate). DEDUP: adjacent to the B176 pad-facet family + B226's pad-at-the-producer face (both are CONSUMER-side padding; this is the STAGING-side mirror angle - unnamed in any record I can read); disclosure: origin is Vulkan compute (chunked dispatch over maxComputeWorkGroupCount) - no our-lane vulkan consumer, transfer is the LAW not the codegen; rider-grade at the pad/tail family slot at the next kernel batch. **(2) LANE INTEL - the upstream NO-CACHE PATH CLUSTER (10-12/13): `358a420e` #30416 pad-KV-to-256 [already B226's rider source] + `9880157` #30439 ggerganov "avoid reallocations in the no cache path" + `ed019cd` #30255 skip-logits-alloc** - upstream is actively polishing the clef/GDN-class no-cache path; the opponent's next league bout build carries all three.
